- 2026-09-10 — Three places where this batch implements LESS than the round-1 report proposed, each argued rather than skipped, so round 2 can judge the reasoning. (1) O-002 proposes giving `vf_semantic_file` the same fence suppression as the authorization scanner "so the two views cannot disagree". Refused: the manifest view should hash MORE, not less. Suppressing fenced Decisions content there would mean an edit hidden inside a code fence no longer invalidates a capture, which weakens freshness to make two parsers cosmetically agree. The authorization scanner ignoring fenced text and the manifest hashing it is the correct asymmetry — one asks "is this in force", the other asks "did anything change". (2) O-004 proposes rejecting `Status: resolved` unless `Prior` names a finding recorded in an earlier round of that gate. Implementing that literally would reject the round-1 report already canonized in notes/opponent.md, whose O-006 resolution refers to the 2026-09-09 provisional review — real, recorded review input that was never a counted round. So the enforced rule is `Prior` must name at least one syntactically valid finding ID and may not be `none`, which blocks the actual attack (a brand-new data-loss finding neutralized as resolved with `Prior: none`) without invalidating legitimate history. (3) O-007 offers two alternatives; taking the second. Changing `vf_report_block`'s accumulation risks altering already-sealed round digests — including round 1's own 1ac95178… — so instead `vf_reports` refuses non-blank content after a completed round's end marker, and completion re-verifies every previously sealed round digest before publishing. That guard also closes O-004's after-marker variant.

O-001 is right that this is wrong: `FM-002 was excluded for this task; see plan 4.2.` and `FM-002 tests/check.sh::x CAP-1` are the same shape, so no rule can separate permitted narrative from a comma-less malformed row. - 2026-09-11 — O-002 reopened a SECOND time and repaired class-wide as T030o12, before spending round 2. The preserved local probe (notes/verification/handoff/round2-self-attack/) reproduced 8 erroneous authorizations in 20 cases against the stop-point source: T030o7 added fence/blockquote suppression to `vf_round_authorized` but kept the one toggled boolean its sibling parsers already used, and a boolean cannot represent a fence. Markdown closes a fenced block only with a run of the SAME character at least as long as the opener and carrying no info string, so an inner ``` inside an outer ````, a `~~~` inside a ```, a four-space-indented run, and a run with an info string are all content — and every one of them flipped the boolean, exposing the quoted waiver as live on both gates and both authorization forms. The repair is one shared awk function `vf_fence` (constant `_VF_AWK_FENCE`) that retains the opener's character and width; all six togglers call it, so the six parsers cannot drift apart again. The probe now reports `cases=20 mismatches=0` with all twelve controls unchanged.

Its plan-table rules also accepted any indentation, so a row or a header inside an indented code block was a definition or reopened a section. Both now use the shared tracker plus a `vf_indented` helper. Checked first that no shipped artifact depends on the old behavior: no tasks.md in 001, 002, 003, the golden example or the template has a fenced task header, and no plan table row is indented.

- 2026-09-11 — Recorded the rule the sweep follows, because it is an asymmetry rather than an oversight, and documented it in templates/verification-contract.md. Validators that ask *is this in force* — round authorizations, canonical round blocks, reality-check matrix rows, plan definition/family rows, task stages and task blocks — suppress fenced, blockquoted and indented content. Validators that ask *what must be covered* — `vf_ac_catalog` and `vf_semantic_file`'s input manifest — deliberately do NOT, because dropping an obligation, or losing an edit hidden inside a fence from a capture's input identity, is the unsafe direction. This generalizes the 2026-09-10 refusal to give `vf_semantic_file` the authorization scanner's suppression.

- 2026-09-11 — HTML-comment suppression must be code-span aware, and checking that BEFORE implementing avoided wedging the spec. A naive whole-line scan for `<!--`/`-->` would enter comment state at notes/opponent.md:211 — round 2's own O-002 Class-wide fix line, which quotes three openers and one closer inside inline code spans — and suppress the remainder of the canonical report, so `vf_reports` would then fail every later reserve, complete, reconcile and probe with no scripted recovery. Across every parsed artifact (003's spec/plan/tasks/STATUS/report, shipped 001 and 002, the golden example and all templates) the only comment tokens outside inline code spans are the two anchored `gate-round-end` markers. The tracker therefore strips inline code spans before looking for comment tokens, exempts the anchored marker, and suppresses a line only when it starts inside a comment or its own content begins with an opener — while a mid-line opener still suppresses the following lines, which is the shape O-002 actually demonstrates.

- 2026-09-11 — Five follow-ups opened for round 2, one per class: T030o13 (O-002, blocking), T030o14 (O-004, blocking), T030o15 (O-005, blocking), T030o16 (O-008, minor) and T030o17 (O-009, minor). The two minors are fixed in the same batch rather than deferred: both are single-rule changes in files the blocking fixes already touch, and leaving a known unmet acceptance ("a report carrying any non-blank line after a round completion marker is refused") unrepaired while spending the last round would be exactly the narrow-enumeration habit this round criticized. `<details>`/raw-HTML blocks, which the report flags as an open question rather than a finding, are deliberately NOT suppressed: their content is rendered and a reviewer reading STATUS sees it, which is the property that distinguishes a record from a hidden instruction.

- 2026-09-11 — Every guard in the round-2 batch is isolated by its own mutation, and the matrix caught two weaknesses in my own fixtures before the gate could. E (no comment suppression) kills the commented waiver, commented task block and commented stage cases; G (Prior by shape) kills both the fabricated-ID negative and the report-vouches-for-itself negative; H (process substitution accepted) kills six configuration negatives; I (after-marker guards removed) kills both new shapes; J (Decisions by prefix) kills the rejected-decisions case. The first control quoted a balanced opener and closer, which a naive scanner also handles; the second added a second prose line that happened to contain a closer and cancelled the first. The discriminating shape is exactly one line carrying an opener with no closer anywhere on it, which is the shape the round-2 report itself has, and only that control fails under F. Recorded because a green mutation is not a passing test, and both misses were in the fixture rather than the fix.

- 2026-09-11 — O-004's repair deliberately does not take the report's own `prior == fid` escape hatch, which any brand-new finding could satisfy by naming itself. The enforced rule is existence in the gate's recorded history: the finding ID in an earlier round, or `Prior` naming an ID from an earlier round, or an ID in a declared `<gate>-findings-<label>.md` prior-review manifest beside the canonical reports. The canonical report and its copies are explicitly not a source, and a fixture asserts that — a report that vouched for the IDs it invented would validate anything. This accepts round 1's `O-006`, whose `Prior` refers to the uncounted 2026-09-09 provisional review recorded in `notes/verification/reports/opponent-findings-2026-09-09.md`, so both sealed rounds still parse and both digests are unchanged.

- 2026-09-11 — Opponent round 3 returned CHALLENGED (3 blocking + 1 minor) and could NOT be canonized, through a defect in my own T030o14. `vf_reports` located the prior-review manifest with `dirname "$file"`, which is correct for the canonical `notes/opponent.md` but wrong for the temporary `combined.md` that `round --complete` validates under `notes/verification/round-check.XXXX/`; the manifest was therefore invisible exactly when a round is sealed, and round 1's legitimate resolved `O-006` was refused. Proven both ways on the identical combined file: it validates with the manifest on the derived path and fails without it. The defect is worse than it looks because it is self-trapping — repairing it edits tracked source, which changes the source identity, so `vf_floor_validate` can no longer return the reserved source and the round cannot be completed as CHALLENGED by any route. - 2026-09-13 — T030o25's acceptance criterion was CORRECTED mid-implementation, before any capture, and the correction is the point of this batch. As first drafted it read "the `LC_*` family is a closed literal list recorded present/unset", taken from the first of the two options round 4 offered. Probing the rule before trusting it showed it does not close the class: a fixed list records `LC_CTYPE` present in a shell that exports it and unset in one that does not, so the digest still moves between two processes reading a byte-identical tree — and `LC_CTYPE`/`LC_ALL` are precisely the names macOS Terminal, OpenSSH SendEnv and CI images export by default. Measured: with the literal list in place, `LC_TELEPHONE` and `LC_FOO` became stable but `LC_CTYPE` and `LC_ALL` still produced different identities. The enforced rule is therefore that MEMBERSHIP is exactly `verification_environment:` with no fixed list beside it, justified name by name: `PATH` is already pinned by each declared tool's resolved path and content digest plus the harness records, `SHELL` is never read because library functions run under an explicit `bash`, `LANG` and the locale categories are not read either since the harness pins `LC_ALL=C` where collation matters, and `TMPDIR` decides where temp files go rather than what a check concludes. This is the exact failure round 3 and round 4 both diagnosed — an acceptance criterion that is itself the wrong rule, implemented faithfully — caught this time by testing the criterion instead of only implementing it.

- 2026-09-16 — The first round-7 floor FAILED with ENOSPC at 11:28:44Z after about 1h33m and sealed nothing; `floor-latest` still names the stale FLOOR-b0sLmN and no FLOOR newer than it exists. Recorded in full because the failure very nearly passed as a success: the harness reported the background command as "exit code 0", but that was the exit code of the trailing `echo` in the command chain, not the floor's, and the `floor.rc` file written expressly to capture the real code could not itself be written because the disk was already full, so it read back empty. Trusting that exit 0 would have reserved round 7 — the only authorized round — against a floor that never sealed, which is the self-trapping state 2026-09-11 recorded. This is the same defect class the spec exists to close, in the invoker's own tooling: a measurement presented as certifying something it never measured, and the second time in two sessions after the `tail` pipeline on 2026-09-16. The rule generalizes beyond pipes — a command chain reports its LAST element's status, so the exit code of the thing being verified must be captured and then READ BACK, and a guard that writes to the same exhausted resource it is guarding is not a guard. Nothing was reserved, no round was consumed and rounds 1-6 keep their digests. The interrupted writer was then reconciled by hand as `vf_lock` requires: it has no stale-lock recovery by design and refuses with "inspect owner and reconcile interrupted writer", its owner token `32048-10915-32218` named a process confirmed dead, no partial `.capture.*` survived the traps, and no floor had been sealed, so the lock was released. Space was reclaimed from regenerable caches only, about 5.6 GB, leaving 13 GB free; Docker was left alone because its daemon still does not answer, exactly as on 2026-09-08, and `Docker.raw` is sparse at 6.1 GB actual, and `Application Support/Claude/vm_bundles` was left alone as application infrastructure rather than cache. Consequence for MET-001: about 1h33m of cold floor is lost outright and the retry pays the cold cost again. Free disk is hereby a checked precondition of a floor on this host, not something discovered ninety minutes in.

- 2026-09-17 — O-004's PREFERRED RULE WAS MEASURED AND REJECTED BEFORE IT WAS IMPLEMENTED, and that measurement is the point of this batch. The round-7 report and the handoff both prefer "stop `resolved` suppressing the count at :1013, count every recorded finding at its recorded tier, reconcile declared totals and verdict against that". Extracted status and severity per finding per round from the real `notes/opponent.md` first: that rule computes `blocking=6` for round 1 against its declared 5, `6/3` for round 2 against `3/2`, and `7/3` for round 3 against `3/1` — so it rejects three rounds whose digests are sealed and cannot be edited without wedging every later reserve, complete and reconcile. Round 4 onward are unaffected. This is the fourth time a proposed rule would have rejected the record that proposed it, and the second time the check caught it before any code was written. The enforced rule is therefore the report's own stated minimum, derived from the predicate rather than from the field: `Status: resolved` asserts a repair no data available to `vf_reports` can corroborate, so it may not buy a CLEARING verdict; "rests on" is read off the same predicate — the suppressed finding is one this gate's own ROUND record already counted, which is history the report making the claim cannot rewrite. A declared prior-review manifest is not a round and was never counted, so a first round may still resolve a manifest-recorded finding and clear, which is the shape the real round-1 O-006 has. The rule is gate-neutral by derivation (`CLEARED` and `READY`), which also gives the reality-check side its first clearing-verdict protection.

- 2026-09-17 — O-002 closed on both halves, the second of which was open by DOCUMENTATION and not by code. The obligation is derived from the whole production the parser enforces — `- <UTC> — <kind>: gate=<g>; round=<n>; after=<n-1>; by=<b>; reason=<r>[; revision=<v>]` — in which every clause has exactly one refusal path and no refusal path may be silent, so a clause added later inherits it. The gate clause at :2303 was the only one taking a bare `next`, and it is also the one needing a DISTINCTION rather than a note added blindly: `gate=<g>` ranges over the gates that exist, so another KNOWN gate is that gate's record and stays correctly silent, while any other value is a malformed candidate and is named with its line. The gate set moved into one library constant (`VF_GATES` plus `vf_is_gate`) so the guard cannot drift from the gates the CLI accepts. Candidate detection also now covers a `<kind>:` written without its trailing space, but only behind the anchored prefix, so the Decisions entry of 2026-09-04 that names `` `waiver:` `` and `` `spec-revision:` `` in code spans is still not a candidate and the refusal list stays readable — checked against the real STATUS.md, whose round-8 denial lists exactly the same five candidates as before. The authoring half is closed where it was actually open: `spec-gate.sh waiver` is now in README's command block and in the round-cap paragraph the four invoker documents share, and the guard that keeps it there DERIVES the subcommand set from the dispatcher in `scripts/spec-gate.sh` instead of enumerating verbs — the enumeration is exactly how `waiver` shipped with a `--help` line and no reader.

- 2026-09-17 — O-010's proposed normalization contained TWO defects that only probing the rule before trusting it could find, and a third that only mutation found. (1) The round-7 report specifies stripping "backticks, asterisks and underscores". Stripping underscores DELETES THE NEEDLE: `LC_` is one of the names the guard searches for, and a claim naming only the `LC_*` family — a complete statement of the superseded rule — escaped a normalizer that stripped it. Measured, then underscores kept; backticks and asterisks still stripped. (2) A bare participle is not a membership claim. This repository writes "`LC_CTYPE` and `LC_ALL` included" meaning "among them", and the first normalizing matcher read `tasks.md`'s own replacement text as the superseded claim, so the reverse direction now requires a passive verb phrase. (3) The negation window began AT the predicate, so "The snapshot no longer includes `PATH`…" — how English negates this proposition in the forward shape — read as the superseded claim; the window now reaches behind the predicate. Scope is taken from the enumeration the manifest itself uses (`git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` filtered through `vf_bookkeeping_path`, minus `STATUS.md`) so it is the manifest set by construction. Correction to the round-7 report, recorded rather than quietly dropped: its `Where` list names `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh:56-62`, which is the GUARD and not a surviving claim — the live tree carried no survivor at round 7 and carries none now.

D1 through D7 isolate the O-010 guards one at a time — whitespace collapse, manifest-derived scope, negation discrimination, the reverse direction, underscore preservation, the passive verb phrase, and the negation window reaching behind the predicate. - 2026-09-18 — The floor's failure mode on this host is MEMORY, not disk, and instrumenting the second attempt REFUTED the obvious explanation rather than confirming it. Attempt 1 ran 2h37m and died inside `spec-evidence.sh` with 18.7 GB free. Attempt 2 carried a 60-second disk/swap sampler and died after only 33 MINUTES with MORE disk than attempt 1 (never below 15.1 GB against a 19.9 GB peak) and FALLING swap pressure (used fell from 3428M to 2571M across its run). A floor that dies sooner under better conditions is not being killed by its own footprint, which is what the first kill's timing had suggested. What both runs share is that each was started as a harness-managed BACKGROUND TASK on a 16 GB machine, and each was terminated with SIGTERM by the host watchdog. Consequence for the next session, and the single most important thing in this entry: do NOT start the floor as a background task from inside the agent harness — run it in a foreground terminal the watchdog does not supervise, for example by typing `! caffeinate -i -m -s bash scripts/spec-gate.sh floor <spec-dir>` so it executes in the user's own shell.

Every one was verified here before acting. Pass 1: the anchor came from `pwd -P`, which under bash 3.2 keeps the case a caller typed while git reports the on-disk case, so a spec path typed in another case made the spec's own captures inputs and a capture went stale during its own execution. My first repair took the anchor from git discovery instead, and pass 2 showed that this obeys git's environment: with `GIT_DIR` exported, as git does for hooks in a linked worktree, `rev-parse --show-prefix` prints an empty prefix (confirmed on this host), and folding read from `core.ignorecase` can be wrong in either direction, including the escape direction on a case-sensitive volume. Rather than patch git's environment, the class was removed. The anchor and `vf_physical_rel` are now string work on physical paths (`cd -P && pwd -P`, never git), and folding is asked of the filesystem itself: a case-swapped spelling of the spec directory names the same inode. Pass 3 then found that this probe saw no letter to swap in a letterless spec name such as `003-2027`, which `new-spec.sh` can produce, and that a symlink spelled as the case swap would pass as folding on a case-sensitive filesystem. Both are fixed by the reviewer-verified change adopted verbatim: climb to the nearest component with a letter, and treat a symlinked swap as an alias. The pass-1 minors were all repaired rather than deferred: the refusal and member skip fold case where the filesystem folds; `..` resolves physically; a declared repository root is refused by name; the contract sentence now states exactly what the code does; and fixtures cover non-canonical declared-directory spellings and a declared symlink loop. - 2026-09-24 — INCIDENT, recorded in full because the invoker caused it: a development run of the T030o41 suites in the live worktree DELETED the live `.specify/stack.yml`. Mechanism, confirmed with a minimal script under `/bin/bash` 3.2.57: a subshell function run as the WHOLE of a command substitution, as `run_rc` runs it, that leaves by `exit` fires its EXIT trap after its locals are unwound, so the trap read the CALLER's `present` — the fixture's last loop value, a relative path — in the repository root. `return` and falling off the end do not leak, and no kernel function shares the defect: the only `exit`s after an EXIT trap there are inside awk programs. Earlier dev runs took the same path harmlessly because the loop's last value named a file that does not exist; no tracked path is missing. Recovery was byte-exact: the working-tree bytes came from the pre-repair reproduction copy, hash `4dc6c67e…`, equal to the `path` record the 08:4x live manifest holds for `.specify/stack.yml` (the index version, `5851cd0b…`, differs, so `git checkout` would have lost the unstaged edits), restored at 09:47 with status `MM` again. Fix: `copy_kit` and the O-010 scan no longer use EXIT traps; each owns one private directory removed on every path, and failure paths return. A regression fixture runs a failing `copy_kit` exactly that way with caller variables naming a sentinel, and the trap-and-`exit` mutant removes the sentinel and fails it. Standing rule from this: exploratory suite runs go in a standalone copy, and a suite run is checked for leaving its repository unchanged. Full record: `notes/verification/reports/t030o41-review.md`.

- 2026-09-24 — Session 4 of T030o41: the review-3 repairs, decided here before any code. Entry state was re-derived from the scripts (`notes/verification/reports/t030o41-entry-audit-4.{sh,log,rc}`, exit 0): source `0ab7f123…`, Decisions `b7c82403…`, HEAD `0d55c62`, analyzer 0 errors with the one task-count warning, no lock and no pending reservation, round 11 refused at exit 4, and the worktree status unchanged by the audit. Three premises were measured first in the scratchpad. Under `/bin/bash` 3.2, an `exit` inside a plain `( … )` within a subshell function leaves that function's EXIT trap unrun, and a variable of the function stays visible after it. A temporary assignment before a function call reaches the function's child processes and is gone afterwards. Git 2.50.1 reads no system configuration at all under `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` included, while an exported `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, or without that flag a `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM`, naming a `core.excludesFile` replaces the default XDG excludes. Decisions: (R3-1) git's repository-local names are cleared for git's own processes, never for the snapshot's shell. `vf_snapshot`'s root check runs through `vf_git`; the index listing and every per-entry `cat-file` run in one inner subshell that clears the names once; `vf_source_paths` clears its own. The environment records then read the ambient values, so a project that declares one of these names under `verification_environment` because its check reads it has that value hashed like any declared name, as "membership is exactly the declaration" requires, and an undeclared one still decides nothing. Declaring one is not refused: a check can genuinely read one, and declaration is the documented route. Fixtures: a declared `GIT_PREFIX` with two values gives two identities and a `present` record; an exported `GIT_DIR` at a missing directory leaves the snapshot's identity equal to the unexported one, a parity that had a fixture only for `vf_source_paths`. Cost: three `--local-env-vars` lookups per snapshot. (R3-3) A fixture that plants git configuration pins all of it through one helper, `git_config_pinned`: `HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` as planted, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` unset. Git's command-line configuration variables (`GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`) are repository-local names, already cleared when a suite starts. The XDG fixture exports a `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and a `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` naming a `core.excludesFile` around both pinned calls, so the pin itself is proven. Unsetting `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` is redundant under `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` on this git, as measured, and is kept so the pin rests on no single variable's precedence; its removal alone is an argued equivalent in the matrix. A search of `tests/` for git configuration (`GIT_CONFIG`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `excludesFile`, `templateDir`) finds that fixture alone; the template fixture's `GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR` outranks `init.templateDir` and reads no configuration. (R3-2) A host-kit fixture binds every load-time alias that names a live-kit directory in its scope, so it judges whatever spelling its site reads, the exact pre-delta one included. A new helper, `on_host_kit`, binds `KIT_DIR` and `SCRIPTS` (the aliases `tests/helpers.sh` defines) to the host for the call. It is used at every call whose host is a whole kit; the golden-example call also binds `EXAMPLE`, and the usage-limits calls also bind `FIXTURES`. The two partial-tree hosts (002's notes for the tally, and the leftover-spec tree) hold no `scripts/`, so they bind `KIT_DIR` alone: their sites read the live kit only through it, and K12 and K31 kill the pre-repair spellings. The load-time file aliases (`ANALYZE`, `MP` and the like) name one tracked file each, which the identity hashes verbatim, so they carry no ignored content and are not bound. The copy sites keep deriving their source from `KIT_DIR` at call time. Matrix K43 and K44 use the exact pre-delta lines, `cp -R "$SCRIPTS" "$DISPATCH_KIT/scripts"` and `cp -R "$EXAMPLE" "$spec_dir"`. (R3-4) `gate_dispatch_grammar_cases` copies through `copy_kit "$KIT_DIR" "$kit" scripts`, with a host-kit fixture carrying an ignored script. The member set was re-derived by search (`t030o41-r34-search.sh`, run as `t030o41-r34-search-pre.{log,rc}`, exit 0). The script lists every load-time alias from the suites' top-level assignments (`KIT_DIR`, `SCRIPTS`, `EXAMPLE` and `FIXTURES` name directories), then prints each of their 38 reads that is bare, globbed, variable-named or directory-valued. Classified by hand, every one goes through the enumeration (`copy_kit`, `kit_files`, `vf_source_paths`, `vf_path_content`), sources one library file, or names one file from a literal or an enumerated list, except `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh:1596`. So the repairs entry's "the five sites" is six, and the contract's "copy kit content only through `copy_kit`" and the task's "used for every copy of live-kit content" hold once this lands. No textual grep guard is added: a guard over spellings is the enumeration mechanism this log records failing, while each member has a behavioral fixture and the search is kept as a re-runnable receipt. T030o41's Acceptance is updated for R3-1 to R3-3 before any code, and its Files list is unchanged. The member splits in three by what varies. (1) The locale categories vary BY DEFAULT between shells, which is the variation the 2026-09-13 rule designed the identity to tolerate. `tr`, `sed`, `cut`, `wc` and `od` read `LC_CTYPE`; `sort` and `find` read `LC_COLLATE`, `LC_TIME` and more; `date` formats `%p` through strftime's `LC_TIME`; awk and grep take character classes from `LC_CTYPE`; bash's own `[[ =~ ]]` uses the C library's regex under the shell's locale; and git's messages follow `LC_MESSAGES`. These are PINNED. Every command in the kit's `reuse_checks` (`tests/run.sh` and `tests/shellcheck.sh`) exports `LC_ALL=C` right after its `unset CDPATH BASH_ENV ENV`, before any other program runs, and so does `tests/helpers.sh`, so a suite run on its own, as a task's Verify runs, is pinned too. `LC_ALL` overrides `LANG` and every category, and gettext ignores `LANGUAGE` in the C locale. `tests/shellcheck.sh` is a member the approved parenthetical did not name. It is the kit's other reuse-checked command, and it was measured: ShellCheck's output is byte-identical, empty and at exit 0 under nine locale settings (C, POSIX, C.UTF-8, en_GB, de_DE, ja_JP in UTF-8 and SJIS, en_US.ISO8859-1, and none set), pinned or not, so there the pin changes nothing and makes that hold by construction. Nothing measured contradicts the approved approach. (2) `TZ` is consulted by `date` and by the kit's local-time arithmetic, but it decided no verdict: with the locale pinned, the full suite passes 147/147 under `TZ=Pacific/Kiritimati`, `Etc/GMT+12` and `America/St_Johns`. It is not pinned. Pinning it would stop the dispatcher's local-time path from running in the zone it runs in, and no measurement asks for it; this is recorded as measured, not proven. (3) Option variables that a program reads only when someone sets them decide verdicts even with the locale pinned (`o016-option-vars.txt`). `UNAME_SYSNAME=Linux` makes macOS `uname` print Linux, and the usage-limits suite then fails 20/27, because the kit branches on `uname`. `GREP_OPTIONS=-v` inverts macOS grep; it broke the suite runner itself, and that run was stopped. The pages also name `TAR_READER_OPTIONS`/`TAR_WRITER_OPTIONS`, `POSIXLY_CORRECT`, `GNUSORT_*` and `COLUMNS`, and `shasum` is a Perl program, which reads `PERL5OPT`. These are the class of the harness-level bash startup environment recorded on 2026-09-24 (`BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `SHELLOPTS`, `BASHOPTS`, exported functions): a setting outside the repository deciding a verdict, not default variation. The pin is placed once: `verification-lib.sh` pins when it loads, and each of the six harness entry scripts loads it directly after `lib.sh`, before any program reads a repository or bookkeeping byte. `spec-run.sh`, `spec-task.sh` and `spec-evidence.sh` move that load up, and `spec-ac-coverage.sh` loads it on every path, not only for `--through-stage`. The measurement called the pin from each entry script and from the kernel; the adopted placement puts the same pin before the same first parser, and the dev run and fixtures re-prove M1 to M5 on the adopted tree. `lib.sh` is unchanged, so 002's dispatcher, which sources it but not the kernel, and the provider CLIs it starts keep the user's locale. The caller's `LC_ALL` (a value, empty, or unset) is kept in the exported `SDD_HARNESS_CALLER_LC_ALL` (`unset` or `set:<value>`), which a harness process started by another inherits (`spec-gate.sh accept` execs `spec-task.sh`; the floor runs `spec-task.sh list`); a process re-reads it only when it starts with no record or with `LC_ALL` other than `C`. The one program the harness runs that is not its own parsing, the captured command (`vf_capture`, and `spec-run.sh`'s legacy run), gets the caller's `LC_ALL` back exactly and never sees the record (M4: unset, empty and `en_US.UTF-8` each reach the command as they were). A declared `LC_ALL` is hashed by the caller's value, as the unrepaired kernel hashed it (M5: identical records for unset, `en_US.UTF-8` and `de_DE.UTF-8`, and two values give two records). A caller who exports the record by hand gets exactly what exporting that `LC_ALL` would give, so it opens nothing. Consequences stated rather than discovered: bash's own matching in the harness is now C as well, so `nocasematch` folds ASCII letters only (a non-ASCII case-variant spelling of a spec's own record is hashed, which fails closed), and the capture loop's glob order is C's, the order every recorded run used (C.UTF-8 collates as C). SECOND HALF, exit statuses. The rule is that no parse failure reads as a passing result. Every harness parser whose output decides a record, an identity or a verdict, and whose failure would read as an absence that passes, reads its exit status and fails closed with a diagnostic naming the parse. The members are derived from every text-program call in the eight files (`t030o43-parser-search.sh`, run as `t030o43-parser-search-pre.{log,rc}`, 151 candidate lines) plus the harness children the kernel reads, classified in `t030o43-parser-classes.md`: M1 the prior-review manifest (`:955`), M2 the Decisions-log authorization parse (`:2465`, which fails closed but then claims no candidate exists), M3 the frontmatter reads that pick the contract, worktree or branch (a failed read selected the legacy path, which ticks a v1 task with `--evidence` alone), M4 the ID inventories that decide coverage (`spec-ac-coverage.sh` exits 0 on an empty inventory), M5 the sorts whose output file the identity or acceptance reads, M6 `spec-task.sh list` in `vf_evidence_current` and the floor, M7 the counts over harness records, M8 the analyzer's clarification markers, M9 `spec-evidence.sh`'s legacy records and artifact names, and M10 the tally's metric lines. Sites whose failure already refuses, display-only sites and warning-only analyzer lines are listed there with the reason. Umbrella-only readers stay recorded extent. `lib.sh`'s own helpers keep their contract for their other callers, and the harness reads the frontmatter it decides with through one checked reader. Grep's decoding failure never shows in its exit status (it stops matching at the byte and exits 0), so for grep the pin is the repair and the status read covers every other failure. The fixtures: the manifest cases refuse exactly as under C with every `LC_*` removed under `en_US.UTF-8`, `de_DE.UTF-8`, `C.UTF-8` and `ja_JP.SJIS`, through `vf_reports` and end to end through `round --complete`; the analyzer's marker and AC inventory, and `spec-ac-coverage.sh`'s AC inventory, refuse the undecodable byte as under C; every harness entry script runs its programs under `LC_ALL=C`; the captured command sees the caller's `LC_ALL` unset, empty or set; a declared `LC_ALL` gives two identities for two caller values; and, through a helper that puts a failing copy of one program first on `PATH` for one argument, a fault injected into each member parser fails its operation closed with its exact diagnostic. On macOS each locale must be one an unpinned awk cannot decode the byte under, or the fixture fails by name. Removing the pin, an entry script's early load, the restore, the caller-value record or any status read fails a fixture. The contract paragraph and the kernel comment are corrected to say that the harness runs its own parsing under `LC_ALL=C`, gives the caller's `LC_ALL` back to the command it captures, and never lets a parse failure read as a pass. Files: `scripts/verification-lib.sh`, `scripts/spec-run.sh`, `scripts/spec-task.sh`, `scripts/spec-gate.sh`, `scripts/sdd-analyze.sh`, `scripts/spec-ac-coverage.sh`, `scripts/spec-evidence.sh`, `templates/verification-contract.md`, `tests/helpers.sh`, `tests/test-verification.sh`, `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh`, `tests/test-spec-gate.sh`, `tests/test-sdd-analyze.sh`, beyond the five-file guideline as T030o41 was, because the members are one class across the harness. Order: the repair; a dev run in a standalone copy (the changed fixtures, then the full suite plainly and under `LANG=en_US.UTF-8` with every `LC_*` removed); one independent review; the mutation matrix; acceptance. Decisions freeze from acceptance through round 13's completion and tally.

- 2026-09-30 — The review-3 repairs, derived from the mechanisms and MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/t030o43-measure-9.{sh,log,rc,start,end}`, exit 0, 343 PASS, the live status outside `notes/verification/` unchanged; run 1, rc 1 on two faults of its own, a check string and a history comparison written as byte identity where the rule intends nine values to change, kept as `-run1`), in four kits built from the live enumeration: CUR, PRE, REV and D (CUR with the candidate pairs `t030o43-edits/kernel-6.pairs`, `spec-task-3.pairs` and `sdd-analyze-3.pairs`, generated from the files' exact lines by `t030o43-edits/genpairs.pl` from the texts in `t030o43-edits/session9-new/`), judged under C and en_US.UTF-8 with every `LC_*` removed; every case of the session-8 measurement of record gives its result in D. THE RULE. (1) Values (R3-F4). The mapped line decides what a line is; every value a member passes on, into a catalog or a record, to another program, or into a comparison with a value that did not come through the step, is taken from the line as written by the anchor the mapped line was matched with, and trimmed at its ends by the same whitespace. The members were derived by listing every value that reaches a `rec`, a `print`, an `emit` or an outside comparison in each of the sixteen programs that take the step: `vf_config`'s stage map; `vf_bindings`' plan cells (split from the row as written, with a whitespace-aware trim), its inventory's stage, its task pass's stage and every field (Files, Verify kind, Tests, Verify, Reuse, Refs, Integration owner) and the maps it reads back (split from the line as written, because the step maps every input line); every identity and finding field value of the main pass of `vf_reports`, its Where continuation and its legacy severity; `spec-task.sh`'s task-list stage and subject; and the analyzer's two stage readers (its umbrella tag pass matches the stage by substring only, so there the change is an argued equivalent). Where a written value is processed further, the tests on it read the same whitespace (`vf_wstrim` as the passes' trim, `vf_wsclass` before a Files entry's `(new)` suffix, `vf_ws` before a Ref's `plan §` test). Not members, each for its reason: the manifest pass and the waiver scanner pass on only values whose grammar admits no whitespace (IDs, Classes, severities, timestamps, a `revision=` compared with a hex digest), which read the same from either line; `vf_ac_catalog`'s text is a canonical form by design, every run of whitespace one space, and `vf_matrix_parse` compares only its criterion cell with it (item 6); a name ends at any whitespace by the recorded R2-F5 rule, so `spec-evidence.sh`'s artifact names and the analyzer's Agent path are read from the mapped line, and a display that quotes an Agent line it cannot read quotes it as read; `spec-evidence.sh`'s stage and the analyzer's AC inventory are matched by substring or by ASCII pattern only; `vf_fm_value` and the STATUS view already read as written. Measured: a Tests locator and its registry row written with a U+00A0 inside the file name bind, `vf_locators_exist` finds the file and the catalog holds it as written; a manual observation name written with a U+00A0 equals the return's; T001 with a U+00A0 after each comma of its Refs, a Files entry ending in a U+00A0 and `(new)`, a Tests value after a U+00A0 and its stage heading written with a U+00A0 on each side of Backend binds, with its stage Backend. (2) The manifest pass's fields (R3-F1, R3-F2). A field is decided by its label: a line whose first word, after indentation, a bullet or ordered-list marker and emphasis, is Class or Severity in any case, and which is an emphasized label or is not continued by a letter, a digit or a hyphen. Inside a finding such a line must be written `- **Class:**` or `- **Severity:**`, appear once, and carry a value of its grammar, which is the text after that label and nothing further; anything else is refused by name. Measured: each of R3-F1's and R3-F2's spellings is refused by name in D under both locales, the ordered-list one included, and review 3's field-rule measurement over every recorded `notes/*.md` of 001, 002 and 003 and every real manifest, with the emphasized-label trigger added, makes no line a candidate that is not a canonical field line. The canonical parser's `value()` takes the text after the FIRST bold label of its line, from the line as written, so a second label stays in the value and fails its grammar: a canonical round's `- **Severity:** data-loss; **Prior:** minor` is refused as an unknown severity, where CUR counted it minor. Measured on the history: every tally, and every field of every recorded report catalog except the free-text Scenario and Reproduced values, are identical in D and CUR; nine such values in 003's opponent report (eight Scenario, one Reproduced), each quoting a bold label, are now the whole text where the greedy reading kept only the part after the last quoted label (each old value a suffix of the new), and no catalog reader reads those two fields (`vf_tally` reads the type, ID, Class, status, Prior, severity and Where). (3) Headings (R3-F3): a line not read as an ATX heading whose text, after leading whitespace or non-ASCII bytes, is a `#` run and the word Finding (any case, not continued by a letter) is refused by name; measured over six spellings after a Class-only finding (a U+00A0 after the hashes; a tab, a U+200B or a U+00A0 before them; no space after them; four spaces of indentation). (4) Outside any finding (R3-F5): the written spelling is refused as before, and any other Class or Severity line is prose until a finding ID appears after the last heading, on the heading or any line, and refused by name from then on. CORRECTION to review 3's proposed fix, which made every non-canonical line outside a finding prose: measured, that fix would drop in silence a finding that combines two R2-F2 spellings, a heading the pass does not open (a word before Finding, a bold Finding line with no heading, a colon after Finding) with unbolded fields, which CUR refuses; the ID condition refuses each of them by name, because only a finding's record can carry a Class or a tier. Measured: R3-F5's summary list and introduction parse; its nested sub-bullet inside a finding stays refused by name as a second Severity line, deliberately, because inside a finding such a line may be the tier. (5) Default field splitting (R3-F6): no member splits a mapped line by awk's default field splitting; the round heading's number, the totals line, the canonical Finding heading's ID and `vf_ac_catalog`'s continuation test read through `vf_fields` (ASCII blanks) or a pattern, and the canonical Finding ID is taken from the line as written, so its refusal names the bytes the author wrote. Measured: the D kit reads a Finding heading and a totals line carrying a U+00A0 identically on macOS awk and in the container's mawk. (6) The matrix criterion (R3-F8): the criterion cell is read by the AC catalog's own whitespace rule before it is compared; measured, a cell written with the U+00A0, an ASCII space or two spaces equals the catalog, and 003's sealed round-1 matrix cells are identical. (7) Stages (R3-F11): every reader of a stage heading that compares or decides with it takes its text as written, trimmed at both ends (`vf_heading_text`): `vf_config`, the inventory, the task pass, `spec-task.sh`'s list and the analyzer; measured end to end through `through-stage` for a trailing U+00A0 and a trailing ASCII space. (8) E02 (R3-F9) becomes a kill row, judged by a checkpoint of a spec whose contract is written with a U+00A0 before `1` and whose task lost a binding, which must be refused on the lost binding. History, D against CUR under both locales: `tally`, the analyzer and `spec-ac-coverage.sh` for 001, 002 and 003, `spec-evidence.sh` for 001 and 002, `spec-task.sh list`, the plan and task binding catalogs and `vf_config`'s stage maps for all three, the report catalogs as above, 003's sealed matrix cells, the round-13 and round-14 waiver checks and every STATUS semantic view are identical; the snapshot records outside the three edited files and the harness records are identical and D gives one digest in both locales; the manifest pass over all 18 recorded notes files and manifests, read as manifests, gives identical records and diagnostics. Recorded, not repaired: R3-F7 and R3-F10 (open extent, entry above); a finding opened by a line with no `#` run inside another finding (a bold Finding line) whose Severity follows a Class-only finding still falls into that finding, which is R3-F3's precondition beyond the heading catch-all and is recorded extent; a heading with no space after its `##` (not a CommonMark heading) is a stage to the inventory and not to `vf_config`, pre-existing. Fixtures: each guard gets an assertion with its exact diagnostic in the suite functions the task already binds (`report_manifest_locale_cases`, `analyzer_locale_and_fault_cases`, `checkpoint_parse_fault_cases`, `harness_locale_cases`), and matrix v4 a kill row for it; a default-split replacement that reads the same bytes on macOS awk (the totals line, the round number, the continuation test) is an argued equivalent, measured on mawk. The task's Files list is unchanged. Next, in order: the task block's Acceptance, corrected before any code (next entry); the pairs files applied, then fixtures and matrix v4; dev runs (quick, then full and full-locale in parallel); one short focused review 4; the matrix of record; freeze, capture, return, accept; one fresh floor on AC; round 13 under the recorded 2026-09-27T15:55:36Z authorization.

- 2026-09-30 — Session 10 of T030o43: the capture of record FAILED, the cause was reproduced, and the user chose the class-wide repair. The entry state (`notes/verification/reports/t030o43-entry-10.txt`) and the freeze point (`t030o43-freeze.txt`) equal `t030o43-handoff-state-9.txt`, the host on AC at entry. CAP-521-Q8OdIp (`t030o43-capture.{log,rc,start,end}`, input `ecbca9d2…`, the frozen source) exited 1; the task notification reported exit 0, which was the wrapper's. `tests/test-sdd-analyze.sh` ran 18/19, failing `test_verification_bindings` on six assertions of T030o43's own fault fixture `analyzer_locale_and_fault_cases`, while every other suite and ShellCheck passed. Nothing was accepted or ticked. The cause, reproduced in a standalone copy (`t030o43-tmpdir-repro.{sh,log,rc,note}`, with the two suite logs; its rc of 1 is the script's own miscount of five assertions for six, see the note): the fixture builds its fault matcher (`fault_bin … "$spec_dir/tasks.md"`) and its expected diagnostics from the caller's spelling of the sandbox path, which begins with TMPDIR. macOS's default TMPDIR ends in `/`, so the sandbox is `…/T//sddkit.*`, while `sdd-analyze.sh:31` canonicalizes its argument with `cd && pwd`, which collapses `//`, so the injected fault never fires and the printed path differs. With TMPDIR written without the slash the suite passes 19/19, which is how every dev run and both shards of the matrix of record set it, so none of them saw it. It falsifies the contract sentence (`templates/verification-contract.md:378`) and the kernel comment (near `scripts/verification-lib.sh:1917`) that TMPDIR decides "never what a check concludes": the kit's own reuse-checked suite concluded by its spelling. It fails closed here, and it is O-016's shape: TMPDIR is undeclared, so a PASS captured under one spelling would be reused by a floor sealed from a shell whose spelling fails a fresh run. - 2026-09-30 — The TMPDIR rule, MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/t030o43-measure-11.sh`; part a `t030o43-measure-11a.{log,rc,start,end}`, exit 0, 35 PASS; part full `t030o43-measure-11full.{log,rc,start,end}` exit 0, 9 PASS, its five suite logs kept as `t030o43-measure-11-<run>.log`; the O-010 scan pre-check `t030o43-o010-precheck.{sh,log,rc}`, exit 0: `superseded_environment_scan` reports nothing on CUR, and nothing on D with this batch's task-block correction applied; runs 1 and 2, rc 127 on a `timeout` macOS lacks and rc 1 on the case file written outside `tests/`, both faults of the pre-check, are kept as `-run1` and `-run2`), in kits built from the live enumeration: CUR (this tree) and D (CUR with `t030o43-edits/helpers-1.pairs`, `tv-5.pairs`, `vc-6.pairs` and `kernel-8.pairs`). THE RULE. Its member is every path a suite makes and every comparison of a path a program prints or receives with the suite's own spelling of it. Every such path begins with TMPDIR, and the programs under test canonicalize a path they are given, lexically (`cd && pwd`, which collapses `//` and resolves `.` and `..`: `sdd-analyze.sh`, `spec-ac-coverage.sh`, `spec-evidence.sh`, `spec-run.sh` and `spec-dispatch.sh` resolve their spec argument so) or physically (`cd -P`, `pwd -P`, as the kernel's anchor has since T030o39). So the rule is stated over the root, not over the comparisons, which are the enumeration this log records failing: when `tests/helpers.sh` loads, right after the locale pin, it sets and exports TMPDIR to the physical path of the directory it names (`cd -P && pwd -P`; `/tmp` when it is unset). The physical path is a fixed point of both canonicalizations, so every spelling of one directory gives a suite one string; it names the same directory, so where temporary files go is unchanged; and a TMPDIR that names no directory stops the suite by name at exit 2. Measured: (A) in D, eight spellings of one directory (as written, a trailing slash, a doubled slash, `/./`, `sub/..`, a symlink, a symlink with a trailing slash, a symlink with `sub/../`) and an unset TMPDIR each export the physical path, where CUR keeps every spelling and leaves an unset one unset; a TMPDIR naming no directory stops D at exit 2 with that diagnostic alone; (B) the member fails in CUR under a trailing slash on exactly its six assertions, among them the fault-injected task-reference read, and passes without one; in D it passes under a trailing slash, `//./`, a symlink with a trailing slash and no TMPDIR; (C) the new fixture passes in D under a trailing slash and unset, and is killed with its diagnostics when D gets CUR's `tests/helpers.sh` back, and, when the canonicalization is only lexical (`cd && pwd`), by the symlink spellings alone; (D) the input identity's records differ between CUR and D only in the four edited files' path and index records and the kernel's harness record; (E) ShellCheck passes on D; (F) the full suite and ShellCheck, each in its own copy left unchanged: in CUR, the census found no member beyond the one fixture, which fails on the same six assertions under a symlinked TMPDIR with a trailing slash, while an unset TMPDIR (`/tmp`, itself a symlink on macOS) passes every suite; in D, the full suite passes at 147/147 with ShellCheck under a trailing slash, under a symlinked TMPDIR with a trailing slash and with no TMPDIR. The contract sentence and the kernel comment are corrected (`vc-6`, `kernel-8`): TMPDIR decides where temporary files go, while its spelling (a trailing or doubled slash, a symlink) can decide a check that compares a path under it with the path a program prints, so the kit's own suites set it to the physical path of the directory it names when they start, and a project's check that does so pins it the same way or declares it. The fixture is in `reuse_command_input_cases` (`tests/test-verification.sh::test_capture_provenance`): the eight spellings, the unset case, the non-directory refusal, and a nested suite under a symlinked TMPDIR with a trailing slash whose sandbox must equal both canonicalizations of itself. Matrix v5 (`t030o43-mutations.sh`, v4 kept as `-v4.sh`) runs every judge under a TMPDIR ending in `/`, adds `tests/helpers.sh` to the byte-identical restore checks and `reuse_command_input_cases` to the baselines, and rows T01 to T06, each a kill: the canonicalization removed, judged by the fixture (T01) and by the member the capture failed on (T02); a lexical canonicalization (T03); the export dropped (T04); the refusal dropped (T05); the `/tmp` default dropped (T06). This session's dev runs use `t030o43-dev10.sh`, a copy of `t030o43-dev.sh` that gives every run a TMPDIR ending in `/`. Recorded, not in scope: a TMPDIR inside a Git worktree is a different directory, not a spelling (review 7's F1, already open extent); Linux, where TMPDIR is usually unset over a physical `/tmp`, was not run here. - 2026-10-01 — T030o44's rule, derived from what renders as a record rather than from the spellings round 13 named, and MEASURED before adoption (`notes/verification/reports/o018-measure.{sh,log,rc,time}`, exit 0, 504 PASS, the live status outside `notes/verification/` unchanged; run 1, rc 1 on two faults of its own comparison, is kept as `o018-measure-run1.*` with a note and the corrected comparison on its own files) on D, which is CUR with `t030o44-edits/kernel-1.pairs` (texts in `t030o44-edits/new/`). The mechanism is that both readers recognise a record line by an enumerated set of spellings and read every other line as prose, which is why four focused reviews of T030o43 and round 13 each found the next spelling. So the rule is stated over what a reader of the rendered Markdown sees and over the record the guards need, and a line a reader would read as a record that the pass cannot read is refused by name. (1) One label reader for both passes, `vf_label` in `_VF_AWK_LABEL`, part of the prelude of every parser of author text: a line carries a Class or Severity label when the first word of its rendered text is that label in any case and the line is an emphasized label or the word is not continued by a letter, digit or hyphen, which is the manifest pass's rule of T030o43 reviews 3 and 4 generalized. The rendered text drops inline raw HTML (comments, open and closing tags, processing instructions and declarations; CommonMark 6.6) and decodes numeric character references (2.5), and its first word is the one after any run of characters that are not letters or digits (indentation, heading markers, list markers, emphasis, backslash escapes), ordered-list numbers and task checkboxes. `vf_finding_heading` reads an ATX heading as a Finding heading when its text is the word Finding, in any case and not continued by a letter or digit, followed, after anything that is not a letter or digit, by a finding ID. (2) The prior-review manifest pass: every finding needs exactly one readable Class and one readable Severity (`finding <ID> has no readable Severity`), which closes at once every route by which a tier goes unread (a heading, inline HTML, a blockquote, a fence, a comment, or no tier at all) instead of naming them; a finding is the section its Finding heading opens, so a heading as shallow as that one closes it and a deeper heading is one of its lines, classified like any other (O-019: a field line under `#### Notes` is read, and a heading that carries a label, `#### Severity: data-loss`, is refused as a Severity line this grammar cannot read); and a heading `vf_finding_heading` reads that the pass cannot open is refused by name. (3) The canonical report pass: a heading `vf_finding_heading` reads, other than `### Finding <ID>`, is refused by name (`a Finding heading this grammar cannot read`) at any level and with any separator, and so is a line not read as a heading that is a `#` run and the word Finding, as the manifest pass already refuses it; a finding is closed by a heading as shallow as `###`, so a field line after a section heading is outside it; a field line outside any finding is refused (`a finding field outside any Finding`), which closes every opener that is not a heading (a bold line, a heading inside a list item) once its fields are written canonically; inside a finding, a line whose label `vf_label` reads as Class or Severity and which is not that field's line is refused (`a Severity line this grammar cannot read in finding <ID>`), so a second tier behind inline HTML, a character reference, a sub-heading or an unbolded label cannot pass the reader; and the duplicate-field key is the field's label, not the line's spelling, so an indented second Severity is a duplicate. Every new canonical rule applies to canonical rounds only and before the completion marker, so legacy rounds and the after-marker refusal are unchanged. Measured, D under C and en_US.UTF-8: every reproduction case is refused by name with its exact diagnostic (the heading and HTML spellings and the setext form as a Severity line this grammar cannot read; the quoted and absent tiers as a finding with no readable Severity; every canonical heading spelling, a U+00A0 before the hashes included, as a Finding heading this grammar cannot read; the bold line and the list-item heading as a finding field outside any Finding; `### Finding — G-042` as an incomplete finding, as on CUR; the indented duplicates as duplicate finding fields; the unread first spelling, a second tier behind `<b>`, a decimal or hexadecimal character reference and a sub-heading as a Severity or Class line this grammar cannot read in that finding); both O-019 shapes and a two-level sub-heading are read, so the CLEARED round is refused on the lowered tier and a CHALLENGED round at the manifest tier parses; the manifest siblings of the rendered text (a decimal and a hexadecimal reference, an inline comment, a second tier behind `<b>`, `### Finding — G-042`) are refused by name; every shipped plausible-manifest shape parses except `class-only`, which the rule turns into a refusal by design; the 2026-09-09 prior-review manifest parses with a catalog identical to CUR's; and canonical findings with a `#### Notes` or `#### Details` sub-heading, a titled heading, the recorded `**Class binding.**` lead-in, prose beginning `Severity was`, a quoted example and Held-up prose after the last finding all parse. History, D against CUR under both locales: `tally`, the analyzer and `spec-ac-coverage.sh` for 001 (tally exit 3, as recorded), 002 and 003 (kit path normalized), every opponent and reality-check report catalog with its diagnostics and exit, the round-13 and round-14 waiver checks, and the 368 snapshot records outside `verification-lib.sh` are identical. A survey of every canonical round first (56 findings in 003's 13 rounds; 001 and 002 hold legacy rounds only) found no Finding-like heading variant, no heading deeper than a finding's, no field line outside a finding or after a section, no duplicate or indented field key and no non-canonical Severity line inside a finding. The full suite on D fails one test only, `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh::test_reports_and_tally` (17/18), on fixture manifests whose findings carry a Class alone (the `class-only` row and the manifests the resolved-claim, Class-drift, `--complete` and `--reconcile` fixtures write at `:1417-1575`), whose refusal now comes before the diagnostic each asserts; every other suite and ShellCheck pass. Those fixtures gain a Severity line, which is the change the task block names. Consequences stated rather than discovered: a line inside a canonical finding that BEGINS with a code span holding a label (``  - `#### Severity: data-loss` is the heading form``) is refused, the safe direction, as the manifest pass already refuses it, so a report author puts a word before the code span. Recorded extent, not in scope: a finding written in prose only, with neither a Finding heading nor a canonical field line, is not a record to either reader, which is the recorded O-004 omission extent; a label written with a named character reference or split by a zero-width character is not read; Status, Prior and the other canonical fields are not label-checked, because only Class and Severity decide a count, a tier or a binding a later round can lower, an unread Status leaves the field missing and the finding incomplete, which already refuses, and a resolved claim already needs the gate's own history. Files: `scripts/verification-lib.sh`, `templates/verification-contract.md`, `tests/test-gate-contracts.sh`, `tests/test-spec-gate.sh`.
